People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I just... can't really make myself care enough to go do any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... no. No, I haven't. I don't... I don't think about things like that. It's just... I'm just tired, like I said. Not... not wanting to hurt myself.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... maybe a month? Give or take. I was... well, before this, I was actually really energized. Just... really driven with everything. But then it just kind of... swung the other way. So, yeah. It's been about a month of feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: During that time before this started, did you feel unusually euphoric or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Um... I suppose so. Earlier in the month, I was just... very upbeat. Really charged, I guess. I felt like I was having these brilliant ideas. But... well, if my husband started questioning my plans, I... I got irritated pretty fast. So, yeah. Maybe a bit of both.\n\nDoctor: During that energized period, did you need much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I suppose so. I was only getting... maybe four hours a night? \n\nBut I didn't feel tired. I'd just wake up and... feel ready to go. I'd start sending work emails, or sketching out event ideas. Or I'd just end up... reorganizing the kitchen. I just didn't need as much rest, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During that energized period, did you feel unusually confident or like you could do things other people couldn’t?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. I felt pretty capable. I was... honestly convinced I could just fix the whole department's morale problem at work. And... sort out all our family routines at home, too. I just felt like... if everyone would just get on board with my plan, I could handle it all. So, yeah. I felt confident.\n\nDoctor: During that energized period, were you talking more than usual?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little. My husband mentioned something about it. And... a couple of people at work, too. They said I was talking really fast... and that they couldn't really get a word in because I kept jumping in. So... yes, I suppose I was.\n\nDoctor: During that energized period, did it cause serious problems at work or home, or lead to hospitalization?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean... I didn't end up in the hospital or anything like that. And... I don't think it caused any major issues at work or home. Just... maybe people were a little annoyed with me sometimes, but... no serious problems, I don't think.\n\nDoctor: About how many days did that unusually energized period last?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. It's hard to remember exactly. Maybe... a few days? I didn't really keep track.\n\nDoctor: Did that unusually energized period last at least 4 days in a row?\n\nPatient: Um... I really couldn't say for sure. Like I said, I wasn't counting. It might have been four days... or maybe just three. I just... I don't know.\n\nDoctor: During that energized period, were you more active or taking on more projects than usual?\n\nPatient: Um... I suppose so. I did... sign up to lead some extra pieces of the morale program at work. And... I guess I volunteered for a few more Latino arts events. It was... definitely more than I'd normally take on, but... yeah. I guess I was just keeping busy.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back to that energized period, what is the longest number of days in a row it lasted?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't have an exact number for you. Like I mentioned, I wasn't keeping track of it. It was just... a few days. I don't really know how else to answer that.\n\nDoctor: Before this past month, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. There was a time before. I think... the longest stretch I can remember was maybe about five months. I just... I felt really low and tired most days. It didn't feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Before this past month, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt unusually energized or unusually irritable for at least 4 days in a row?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not that I can think of. Just... just the one time recently.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
